## Semantic Chunks
### Section: Introduction
- **Level**: `intro`
- **Hash**: `0fe7839122da8bc9c529c685f480cd18c5d775d17f8a46e6ba4abf099993ec8c`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/can-an-essay-be-partially-ai-generated` (Similarity: 0.9394)

```text
Yes. Teachers can detect partial AI use in an essay with high precision—because modern integrity platforms analyze text at the sentence and paragraph level rather than treating the essay as a single block. By evaluating sentence-by-sentence perplexity heatmaps, syntactic burstiness shifts, and keystroke writing playback telemetry in Checkmark, educators can pinpoint the exact sentences or paragraphs generated by AI within an otherwise human paper.

Many students believe that mixing human writing with AI generation will fool detection systems: "If I write 70% of the essay myself and only use ChatGPT for the hard 30%, the detector will get confused and average it out." While crude, first-generation AI checkers output a single ambiguous document score, advanced multi-signal platforms evaluate granular sentence-level probability . An essay with three human paragraphs followed by one AI-generated analysis section lights up with pinpoint accuracy. Checkmark Plagiarism pairs sentence heatmaps with keystroke playback to prove partial AI use conclusively.

Below is a comprehensive guide on how teachers detect and evaluate partial AI writing in student submissions.

Checkmark Plagiarism detects partial AI use by pairing AI detection with essay writing playback , plagiarism detection , autograding , and integrations with Canvas and Google Classroom .
```

### Section: The 4 Forensic Signals of Partial AI Use
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `9aec79e07b67b7e7413f5624255aa9838bea22efbea3d516961d941002bd9d0c`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/can-teachers-detect-manually-typed-ai-responses` (Similarity: 0.8933)

```text
1. Sentence-Level Perplexity Heatmaps

Checkmark evaluates each sentence independently, highlighting low-perplexity AI sentences in bright red while leaving authentic human sentences clear.

2. Abrupt Syntactic Burstiness Shifts

The document suddenly shifts from varied, organic human sentence lengths (4 to 35 words) to uniform 22-word synthetic AI clause structures.

3. Keystroke Telemetry Segmentation

Writing Playback shows paragraphs 1–3 were typed over 2.5 hours with 20% backspaces, while paragraph 4 entered in a single 0.05-second clipboard paste.

4. Isolated Lexical Tier Spikes

A sudden influx of doctoral-level vocabulary and AI clichés ( "delve," "tapestry," "pivotal" ) appears only in the AI-assisted sections.
```

### Section: How Multi-Signal Classifiers Pinpoint Hybrid Writing
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `b3beb8d5b8332995ddbce5c9cd55df85ceec6cd82509b8f14083284c12765b9d`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/can-an-essay-be-partially-ai-generated` (Similarity: 0.8962)

```text
Understanding the mathematical and behavioral mechanics behind partial detection:

Sliding-Window Neural Analysis: Rather than taking a global average, Checkmark uses a sliding window of tokens to calculate local perplexity across every paragraph. 
 Corroborating Telemetry Timestamps: When a low-perplexity sentence coincides with a paste event in Playback, the probability of AI generation reaches near-100% certainty. 
 Eliminating False Accusations: Teachers can see exactly which parts of the paper were authored by the student, allowing them to praise genuine human labor while addressing the AI shortcut.

Read more in how Checkmark writing process analysis works .
```

### Section: Comparison: Document-Level Average vs. Checkmark Sentence Heatmap
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `a1a42d0e42a5cb732e49c1e2809f30db1e99ab2b9a75feda70c06f783146f557`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/how-can-teachers-detect-ai-use-without-copy-and-paste-evidence` (Similarity: 0.8266)

```text
Checkmark Sentence Heatmap (Precision Forensics)

Highlights exact AI sentences and paragraphs. 
 Displays exact percentage breakdown (e.g., 32% AI). 
 Synchronized with writing playback keystrokes. 
 Protects the student's authentic human sections.

Document-Level Average (Unclear / Flawed)

Outputs a single ambiguous number (e.g., "45% AI"). 
 Leaves teacher guessing which sections were generated. 
 Risk of falsely accusing entire paper of being fake. 
 Easily contested during formal grade appeals.
```

### Section: A 5-Step Educator Protocol for Partial AI Audits
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `8b0b6025c42909e953acb1a32783bdc027aa1d4badf25b2ab680c27cc1b4f1ec`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/what-if-only-one-paragraph-was-written-by-ai` (Similarity: 0.9443)

```text
Partial AI Audit Checklist:

1. Open the student's submission in Checkmark Plagiarism inside Canvas SpeedGrader. 
 2. Review the Sentence Heatmap: note which specific paragraphs light up with high AI probability. 
 3. Inspect the Writing Playback timeline: verify whether the flagged paragraphs correspond to clipboard paste events. 
 4. Check if the remaining paragraphs show genuine multi-hour human typing with healthy backspaces. 
 5. Hold a 2-minute conference: show the heatmap replay and guide the student to rewrite the flagged section.
```
